(282)

In the towers of Clebsch-Gordan coefficients of the first equality, one can use the orthogonality properties to reduce this expression to
\mathchoice{\delta^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.17659pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.17659pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.23845pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.56793pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{r_{2j}}}}_{{{r_{2j}}\mathchoice{\makebox[9.17659pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.17659pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.23845pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.56793pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\delta^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.17659pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.17659pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.23845pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.56793pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{r_{2j}}}}_{{{r_{2j}}\mathchoice{\makebox[9.17659pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.17659pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.23845pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.56793pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\delta^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.17659pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.17659pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.23845pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.56793pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{r_{2j}}}}_{{{r_{2j}}\mathchoice{\makebox[9.17659pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.17659pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.23845pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.56793pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\delta^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.17659pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.17659pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.23845pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.56793pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{r_{2j}}}}_{{{r_{2j}}\mathchoice{\makebox[9.17659pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.17659pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.23845pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.56793pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}=2j+1
. To simplify the expression on the second line, the cycle structure for the permutations in the symmetrization play a role. For each term in the symmetrization, the contractions evaluate to
2^{k}
with
k
the number of fixed points (disjoint cycles) in that particular permutation. In the contraction of the Kronecker deltas, each fixed point contracts to a trace of one Kronecker delta, which yields a factor of 2 as the
d_{i},e_{i}
indices are associated with spin-1/2.
With these steps, we obtain for Eq. (
282
) that

2j+1=\frac{1}{(2j)!}\sum_{k}c(2j,k)2^{k}\,,

(283)

where
c(n,k)
is the number of permutations of
n
objects with
k
cycles, which correspond to the unsigned Stirling numbers of the first kind
[
57
]
, and where all permutations sum to
\sum_{k=1}^{n}c(n,k)=n!
. These unsigned Stirling numbers obey the generating equation

x^{\bar{n}}\equiv x(x+1)\cdots(x+n-1)=\sum_{k=0}^{n}c(n,k)x^{k}\,.

(284)

Evaluating this generating equation for
x=2,n=2j
recovers Eq. (
283
). This completes the proof.

Appendix E
Useful relations among the reduction tensors

Here we summarize a number of important algebraic relations satisfied by the invariants rank-4 coefficient tensors defined in Eqs. (
103
), (
105
), (
118
) and by the covariant rank-3 coefficient tensors defined in Eqs. (
111
) and (
117
).
Their defining equations are repeated here for convenience,

\displaystyle{\mathcal{C}}^{\mu\rho\sigma\alpha}

\displaystyle=g^{\mu\rho}g^{\sigma\alpha}-g^{\mu\sigma}g^{\rho\alpha}+g^{\mu\alpha}g^{\rho\sigma}+{\rm i}\epsilon^{\mu\rho\sigma\alpha}\,,

(285a)

\displaystyle\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathcal{C}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{\mu\rho\sigma\alpha}

\displaystyle=g^{\mu\rho}g^{\sigma\alpha}-g^{\mu\sigma}g^{\rho\alpha}+g^{\mu\alpha}g^{\rho\sigma}-{\rm i}\epsilon^{\mu\rho\sigma\alpha}\,,

(285b)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{C}}^{\mu\rho\sigma\alpha}_{\text{red}}

\displaystyle=-g^{\mu\sigma}g^{\rho\alpha}+g^{\mu\alpha}g^{\rho\sigma}+{\rm i}\epsilon^{\mu\rho\sigma\alpha}\,,

(285c)

\displaystyle\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathcal{C}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{\mu\rho\sigma\alpha}_{\text{red}}

\displaystyle=-g^{\mu\sigma}g^{\rho\alpha}+g^{\mu\alpha}g^{\rho\sigma}-{\rm i}\epsilon^{\mu\rho\sigma\alpha}\,,

(285d)

and

\displaystyle{\mathcal{Q}}^{\mu\rho\alpha}={\mathcal{C}}^{\mu\rho\sigma\alpha}\eta_{\sigma}

\displaystyle=g^{\mu\rho}\eta^{\alpha}-g^{\rho\alpha}\eta^{\mu}+g^{\mu\alpha}\eta^{\rho}+{\rm i}\epsilon^{\mu\rho\sigma\alpha}\eta_{\sigma}\,,

(286a)

\displaystyle\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathcal{Q}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{\mu\rho\alpha}=\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathcal{C}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{\mu\rho\sigma\alpha}\eta_{\sigma}

\displaystyle=g^{\mu\rho}\eta^{\alpha}-g^{\rho\alpha}\eta^{\mu}+g^{\mu\alpha}\eta^{\rho}-{\rm i}\epsilon^{\mu\rho\sigma\alpha}\eta_{\sigma}\,,

(286b)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{Q}}_{\text{red}}^{\mu\rho\alpha}={\mathcal{C}}_{\text{red}}^{\mu\rho\sigma\alpha}\eta_{\sigma}

\displaystyle=-g^{\rho\alpha}\eta^{\mu}+g^{\mu\alpha}\eta^{\rho}+{\rm i}\epsilon^{\mu\rho\sigma\alpha}\eta_{\sigma}\,,

(286c)

\displaystyle\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathcal{Q}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{\text{red}}^{\mu\rho\alpha}=\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathcal{C}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{\text{red}}^{\mu\rho\sigma\alpha}\eta_{\sigma}

\displaystyle=-g^{\rho\alpha}\eta^{\mu}+g^{\mu\alpha}\eta^{\rho}-{\rm i}\epsilon^{\mu\rho\sigma\alpha}\eta_{\sigma}\,,

(286d)

where,
\eta^{\sigma}\equiv(1,0,0,0)
, see Eq. (
108
).

Many calculations involve the invariant
\mathcal{C}
-coefficients under permutation of the first three indices. For these permutations it is advantageous to relate them back to the ordering used in Eq. (
103
).
The coefficient tensors satisfy the following indices permutations

\displaystyle{\mathcal{C}}^{\sigma\rho\mu\alpha}

\displaystyle=\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathcal{C}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{\mu\rho\sigma\alpha}=\left({\mathcal{C}}^{\mu\rho\sigma\alpha}\right)^{*}\,,

(287a)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{C}}_{\text{red}}^{\rho\mu\sigma\alpha}

\displaystyle={\mathcal{C}}_{\text{red}}^{\mu\rho\alpha\sigma}=-{\mathcal{C}}_{\text{red}}^{\mu\rho\sigma\alpha}\,,

(287b)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{Q}}^{\mu\alpha\rho}

\displaystyle=\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathcal{Q}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{\mu\rho\alpha}=\left({\mathcal{Q}}^{\mu\rho\alpha}\right)^{*}\,,

(287c)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{Q}}_{\text{red}}^{\rho\mu\alpha}

\displaystyle=-{\mathcal{Q}}_{\text{red}}^{\mu\rho\alpha}\,.

(287d)

The analogous identities for
\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathcal{C}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu,\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathcal{C}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{\text{red}},\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathcal{Q}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{\text{red}}
-coefficients are obtained by barring all previous equations, which corresponds, in this case, to taking the complex conjugate.
Note that Eqs. (
287b
) and (
287d
) shows that both
{\mathcal{C}}_{\text{red}}^{\rho\mu\sigma\alpha}
and
{\mathcal{Q}}_{\text{red}}^{\mu\rho\alpha}
are antisymmetric in their first two indices. This plays a role in the permutation symmetry of the Lorentz indices in the quadratic products of
t
-tensors Eq. (
116
), where they generate the bi-index structure of Eqs. (
115
).

The
{\mathcal{Q}}_{\text{red}}
and
\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathcal{Q}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{\text{red}}
also satisfy the following useful relations:

\displaystyle\eta_{\alpha}{\mathcal{Q}}_{\text{red}}^{\mu\rho\alpha}

\displaystyle=\eta_{\alpha}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathcal{Q}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{\text{red}}^{\mu\rho\alpha}=0\,,

(288a)

\displaystyle g_{\mu\rho}{\mathcal{Q}}_{\text{red}}^{\mu\rho\alpha}

\displaystyle=g_{\mu\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathcal{Q}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{\text{red}}^{\mu\rho\alpha}=0\,,

(288b)

and are (anti-)self-dual with regard to the first two indices

\displaystyle\frac{\rm i}{2}\epsilon^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}\mathchoice{(\mathcal{Q}_{\text{red}})^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.78293pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.78293pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.3333pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.80948pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha}}}_{{{\rho\sigma}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{(\mathcal{Q}_{\text{red}})^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.78293pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.78293pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.3333pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.80948pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha}}}_{{{\rho\sigma}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{(\mathcal{Q}_{\text{red}})^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.78293pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.78293pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.3333pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.80948pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha}}}_{{{\rho\sigma}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{(\mathcal{Q}_{\text{red}})^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.78293pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.78293pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.3333pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.80948pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha}}}_{{{\rho\sigma}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}=-\mathcal{Q}_{\text{red}}^{\mu\nu\alpha}\,,

(289a)

\displaystyle\frac{\rm i}{2}\epsilon^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}\mathchoice{(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathcal{Q}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{\text{red}})^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.78293pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.78293pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.3333pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.80948pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha}}}_{{{\rho\sigma}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathcal{Q}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{\text{red}})^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.78293pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.78293pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.3333pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.80948pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha}}}_{{{\rho\sigma}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathcal{Q}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{\text{red}})^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.78293pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.78293pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.3333pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.80948pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha}}}_{{{\rho\sigma}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathcal{Q}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{\text{red}})^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.78293pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.78293pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.3333pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.80948pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha}}}_{{{\rho\sigma}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}=+\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathcal{Q}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{\text{red}}^{\mu\nu\alpha}\,.

(289b)

From Eq. (
288a
) we find that only spatial indices contribute in contractions in the last index of
{\mathcal{Q}}_{\text{red}}^{\mu\rho\alpha}
, which is an important property used in Sec.
V.3
when contracting with
t
-tensors. Their (anti-)self-dual nature helps in making the connections with the SL(2,
\mathbb{C}
) irrep content of certain objects encountered in the constructions in this work.

The algebra among
t
-tensors, i.e. the reduction formulas for products of them, relies on contractions between the covariant tensors in Eqs. (
285
) and (
286
).
Some of these contractions can be simplified by making use of the identity

\epsilon^{\mu\nu\rho\beta}=\eta^{\mu}\epsilon^{0\nu\rho\beta}+\eta^{\nu}\epsilon^{\mu 0\rho\beta}+\eta^{\rho}\epsilon^{\mu\nu 0\beta}+\eta^{\beta}\epsilon^{\mu\nu\rho 0}\,.

(290)

Below we state relations involving unbarred tensors.
As stated above, the analogous equations with barred tensors are obtained by complex conjugation.
The most important contraction between
\mathcal{Q}
-tensors is

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}^{\mu_{1}\rho_{1}\alpha}{\mathcal{Q}}^{\mu_{2}\rho_{2}}{}_{\alpha}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{C}^{\mu_{1}\rho_{1}\rho_{2}\mu_{2}}\,.

(291)

The
\mathcal{Q}_{\text{red}}
-tensors further satisfy

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{\text{red}}^{\mu_{1}\rho_{1}\alpha}\mathcal{Q}_{\text{red}}^{\mu_{2}\rho_{2}}{}_{\alpha}

\displaystyle=-\mathcal{C}_{\text{red}}^{\mu_{1}\rho_{1}\mu_{2}\rho_{2}}\,,

(292a)

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{\text{red}}^{\mu_{1}\rho_{1}}{}_{\alpha_{1}}\mathcal{Q}_{\text{red}}^{\mu_{2}\rho_{2}}{}_{\alpha_{2}}\mathcal{Q}_{\text{red}}^{\alpha_{1}\alpha_{2}\xi}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{Q}_{\text{red}}^{\mu_{1}\rho_{1}}{}_{\alpha_{1}}\mathcal{Q}_{\text{red}}^{\mu_{2}\rho_{2}}{}_{\alpha_{2}}\ {\rm i}\eta_{\alpha_{3}}\epsilon^{\alpha_{1}\alpha_{2}\alpha_{3}\xi}

\displaystyle=g^{\mu_{1}\mu_{2}}\mathcal{Q}_{\text{red}}^{\rho_{1}\rho_{2}\xi}-g^{\mu_{1}\rho_{2}}\mathcal{Q}_{\text{ref}}^{\rho_{1}\mu_{2}\xi}+g^{\rho_{1}\rho_{2}}\mathcal{Q}_{\text{red}}^{\mu_{1}\mu_{2}\xi}-g^{\rho_{1}\mu_{2}}\mathcal{Q}_{\text{red}}^{\mu_{1}\rho_{2}\xi}\,.

(292b)

Note that with Eqs. (
292b
) and (
120
), the Lie algebra commutation relations for the spin
j
representation can be written as

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{\text{red}}^{\mu_{1}\rho_{1}}{}_{\alpha_{1}}\mathcal{Q}_{\text{red}}^{\mu_{2}\rho_{2}}{}_{\alpha_{2}}\mathcal{Q}_{\text{red}}^{\alpha_{1}\alpha_{2}\xi}t_{\xi 0\cdots 0}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\rm i}{j}\left(g^{\mu_{1}\mu_{2}}\mathbb{M}^{\rho_{1}\rho_{2}}-g^{\mu_{1}\rho_{2}}\mathbb{M}^{\rho_{1}\mu_{2}}+g^{\rho_{1}\rho_{2}}\mathbb{M}^{\mu_{1}\mu_{2}}-g^{\rho_{1}\mu_{2}}\mathbb{M}^{\mu_{1}\rho_{2}}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{j}\left[\mathbb{M}^{\mu_{1}\rho_{1}},\mathbb{M}^{\mu_{2}\rho_{2}}\right]\,.

(293)

Introducing the following definition

\tilde{g}^{\beta_{1}\beta_{2}}=g^{\beta_{1}\beta_{2}}-\eta^{\beta_{1}}\eta^{\beta_{2}}\,,

(294)

the other kind of contractions among
\mathcal{Q}_{\text{red}}
-tensors can be written as

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{\text{red}}^{\mu\rho\beta}\left(\mathcal{Q}^{\text{red}}_{\mu\rho}{}\right)^{\alpha}=

\displaystyle 4\tilde{g}^{\alpha\beta}\,,

(295a)

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{\text{red}}^{\mu_{1}\rho_{1}\alpha_{1}}\left(\mathcal{Q}^{\text{red}}_{\mu_{1}}{}\right)^{\rho_{2}\alpha_{2}}=

\displaystyle\tilde{g}^{\rho_{1}\rho_{2}}\tilde{g}^{\alpha_{1}\alpha_{2}}-\tilde{g}^{\rho_{1}\alpha_{2}}\tilde{g}^{\rho_{2}\alpha_{1}}+\tilde{g}^{\rho_{1}\alpha_{1}}\tilde{g}^{\rho_{2}\alpha_{2}}

(295b)

\displaystyle+\eta^{\rho_{1}}\eta^{\rho_{2}}\tilde{g}^{\alpha_{1}\alpha_{2}}+{\rm i}\eta^{\rho_{1}}\eta_{\sigma}\epsilon^{\sigma\alpha_{1}\rho_{2}\alpha_{2}}+{\rm i}\eta^{\rho_{2}}\eta_{\sigma}\epsilon^{\sigma\alpha_{2}\rho_{1}\alpha_{1}}\,.

(295c)

Below we include contractions between
{\mathcal{C}}_{\text{red}}^{\rho\mu\sigma\alpha}
and 2 to 4 copies of
{\mathcal{Q}}_{\text{red}}^{\mu\rho\alpha}
.
As the last index of the
{\mathcal{Q}}_{\text{red}}^{\mu\rho\alpha}
is contracted with a
t
-tensor, we can use symmetrization in this last index to produce simpler results than the more general contractions.
These results are

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\underset{\left\{(\beta)\right\}}{\mathcal{S}}{\mathcal{C}}^{\text{red }}_{\mu_{1}\rho_{1}\mu_{2}\rho_{2}}{\mathcal{Q}}_{\text{red}}^{\mu_{1}\rho_{1}\beta_{1}}{\mathcal{Q}}_{\text{red}}^{\mu_{2}\rho_{2}\beta_{2}}=

\displaystyle\;4^{2}(\eta^{\beta_{1}}\eta^{\beta_{2}}-g^{\beta_{1}\beta_{2}})=-4^{2}\ \tilde{g}^{\beta_{1}\beta_{2}}\,,

(296a)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{3!}\underset{\left\{(\beta)\right\}}{\mathcal{S}}{\mathcal{C}}^{\text{red }}_{\mu_{1}\rho_{1}\mu_{2}\mu_{3}}{\mathcal{Q}}_{\text{red}}^{\mu_{1}\rho_{1}\beta_{1}}{\mathcal{Q}}_{\text{red}}^{\mu_{2}\rho_{2}\beta_{2}}{\mathcal{Q}}_{\text{red}}^{\mu_{3}\rho_{3}\beta_{3}}=

\displaystyle\;\frac{1}{3!}\underset{\left\{(\beta)\right\}}{\mathcal{S}}\left(-4\tilde{g}^{\beta_{1}\beta_{2}}\mathcal{Q}_{\text{red}}^{\rho_{2}\rho_{3}\beta_{3}}\right)\,,

(296b)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4!}\underset{\left\{(\beta)\right\}}{\mathcal{S}}{\mathcal{C}}^{\text{red }}_{\mu_{1}\mu_{2}\mu_{3}\mu_{4}}{\mathcal{Q}}_{\text{red}}^{\mu_{1}\rho_{1}\beta_{1}}{\mathcal{Q}}_{\text{red}}^{\mu_{2}\rho_{2}\beta_{2}}{\mathcal{Q}}_{\text{red}}^{\mu_{3}\rho_{3}\beta_{3}}{\mathcal{Q}}_{\text{red}}^{\mu_{4}\rho_{4}\beta_{4}}

\displaystyle={\mathcal{C}}_{\text{red }}^{\rho_{1}\rho_{2}\rho_{3}\rho_{4}}\frac{1}{4!}\underset{\left\{(\beta)\right\}}{\mathcal{S}}\left(\tilde{g}^{\beta_{1}\beta_{2}}\tilde{g}^{\beta_{3}\beta_{4}}\right)\,.

(296c)

Including the contractions with the
t
-tensors, we then have

\displaystyle{\mathcal{C}}^{\text{red }}_{\mu_{1}\rho_{1}\mu_{2}\rho_{2}}{\mathcal{Q}}_{\text{red}}^{\mu_{1}\rho_{1}\beta_{1}}{\mathcal{Q}}_{\text{red}}^{\mu_{2}\rho_{2}\beta_{2}}t_{\beta_{1}\beta_{2}\cdots}

\displaystyle=16\ t_{00\cdots}\,,

(297a)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{C}}^{\text{red }}_{\mu_{1}\rho_{1}\mu_{2}\mu_{3}}{\mathcal{Q}}_{\text{red}}^{\mu_{1}\rho_{1}\beta_{1}}{\mathcal{Q}}_{\text{red}}^{\mu_{2}\rho_{2}\beta_{2}}{\mathcal{Q}}_{\text{red}}^{\mu_{3}\rho_{3}\beta_{3}}t_{\beta_{1}\beta_{2}\beta_{3}\cdots}

\displaystyle=4\ \mathcal{Q}_{\text{red}}^{\rho_{2}\rho_{3}\beta}t_{\beta 00\cdots}\,,

(297b)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{C}}^{\text{red }}_{\mu_{1}\mu_{2}\mu_{3}\mu_{4}}{\mathcal{Q}}_{\text{red}}^{\mu_{1}\rho_{1}\beta_{1}}{\mathcal{Q}}_{\text{red}}^{\mu_{2}\rho_{2}\beta_{2}}{\mathcal{Q}}_{\text{red}}^{\mu_{3}\rho_{3}\beta_{3}}{\mathcal{Q}}_{\text{red}}^{\mu_{4}\rho_{4}\beta_{4}}t_{\beta_{1}\beta_{2}\beta_{3}\beta_{4}\cdots}

\displaystyle={\mathcal{C}}_{\text{red }}^{\rho_{1}\rho_{2}\rho_{3}\rho_{4}}t_{0000\cdots}\,.

(297c)

Other contractions that frequently appear are the full contractions

\displaystyle{\mathcal{C}}_{\mu_{1}\mu_{2}\mu_{3}\mu_{4}}{\mathcal{C}}^{\mu_{1}\mu_{2}\mu_{3}\mu_{4}}

\displaystyle=64\,,

(298a)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{C}}_{\mu_{1}\mu_{2}\mu_{3}\mu_{4}}{\mathcal{C}}_{\text{red }}^{\mu_{1}\mu_{2}\mu_{3}\mu_{4}}

\displaystyle=48\,,

(298b)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{C}}^{\text{red }}_{\mu_{1}\mu_{2}\mu_{3}\mu_{4}}{\mathcal{C}}_{\text{red }}^{\mu_{1}\mu_{2}\mu_{3}\mu_{4}}

\displaystyle=48\,,

(298c)

and the partial contractions

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}^{\text{red}}_{\mu_{1}\rho_{1}}{}^{\mu_{2}\rho_{2}}\mathcal{C}_{\text{red}}^{\mu_{1}\rho_{1}\mu_{4}\rho_{4}}=

\displaystyle-4\mathcal{C}_{\text{red}}^{\mu_{2}\rho_{2}\mu_{4}\rho_{4}}\,,

(299a)

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}^{\text{red}}_{\mu_{1}}{}^{\rho_{1}}{}_{\mu_{2}}{}^{\rho_{2}}\mathcal{C}_{\text{red}}^{\mu_{1}\mu_{2}\mu_{4}\rho_{4}}=

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{\text{red}}^{\rho_{1}\rho_{2}\mu_{4}\rho_{4}}\,,

(299b)

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}^{\text{red}}_{\mu_{1}\rho_{1}\mu_{2}}{}^{\rho_{2}}\mathcal{C}_{\text{red}}^{\mu_{1}\mu_{2}\mu_{3}\rho_{1}}=

\displaystyle 12g^{\rho_{2}\mu_{3}}\,,

(299c)

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}^{\text{red}}_{\mu_{1}}{}^{\rho_{1}}{}_{\mu_{2}\mu_{3}}\mathcal{C}_{\text{red}}^{\mu_{1}\mu_{2}\mu_{3}\mu_{4}}=

\displaystyle\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathcal{C}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{\text{red}}_{\mu_{1}\mu_{2}\mu_{3}}{}^{\rho_{1}}\mathcal{C}_{\text{red}}^{\mu_{1}\mu_{2}\mu_{3}\mu_{4}}=3g^{\rho_{1}\mu_{4}}\,.

(299d)

The
{\mathcal{R}}^{\mu\sigma\alpha}
tensors were introduced in Sec.
V.2.1
in the reduction of quadratic products
tt
and
\bar{t}\bar{t}
. They are defined in Eq. (
113
) as

\displaystyle{\mathcal{R}}^{\mu\sigma\alpha}={\mathcal{C}}^{\mu\rho\sigma\alpha}\eta_{\rho}

\displaystyle=\eta^{\mu}g^{\sigma\alpha}-\eta^{\alpha}g^{\mu\sigma}+\eta^{\sigma}g^{\mu\alpha}+{\rm i}\epsilon^{\mu\rho\sigma\alpha}\eta_{\rho}\,,

(300a)

\displaystyle\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathcal{R}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{\mu\sigma\alpha}=\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathcal{C}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{\mu\rho\sigma\alpha}\eta_{\rho}

\displaystyle=\eta^{\mu}g^{\sigma\alpha}-\eta^{\alpha}g^{\mu\sigma}+\eta^{\sigma}g^{\mu\alpha}-{\rm i}\epsilon^{\mu\rho\sigma\alpha}\eta_{\rho}=\left({\mathcal{R}}^{\mu\sigma\alpha}\right)^{*}\,.

(300b)

All their properties can be derived from the relations

\displaystyle{\mathcal{R}}^{\mu\sigma\alpha}={\mathcal{Q}}^{\alpha\sigma\mu}\,,

(301a)

\displaystyle\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathcal{R}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{\mu\sigma\alpha}=\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathcal{Q}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{\alpha\sigma\mu}\,.

(301b)

Here we only stress the most relevant for our purposes

{\mathcal{R}}^{\sigma\mu\alpha}=\left({\mathcal{R}}^{\mu\sigma\alpha}\right)^{*}=\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathcal{R}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{\mu\sigma\alpha}\,,

(302)

which means that under an interchange of the first two indices the real part of
{\mathcal{R}}
is symmetric, and its imaginary part is anti-symmetric.
In addition we have,

\displaystyle\eta_{\mu}{\mathcal{R}}^{\mu\sigma\alpha}=g^{\sigma\alpha}\,,

(303a)

\displaystyle\eta_{\sigma}{\mathcal{R}}^{\mu\sigma\alpha}=g^{\mu\alpha}\,.

(303b)

Appendix F
Generalized quadratic reductions

In this section, we provide useful results related to products and (anti)commutation relations among particular cases of
t
-tensors. By explicit calculation, we find

\displaystyle t_{\alpha_{1}\cdots\alpha_{m}0\cdots 0}t_{\beta_{1}0\cdots 0}=

\displaystyle\left(\prod_{l=m+1}^{2j}\eta^{\alpha_{l}}\right)\left(\prod_{r=2}^{2j}\eta^{\beta_{r}}\right)t_{(\alpha)}{t}_{(\beta)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2j}\left[\left(2j-m\right)t_{\alpha_{1}\cdots\alpha_{m}\beta_{1}0\cdots 0}+\sum_{n=1}^{m}{\mathcal{R}}_{\alpha_{n}\beta_{1}\xi}t^{\xi}_{\alpha_{1}\cdots\alpha_{n-1}\alpha_{n+1}\cdots\alpha_{m}0\cdots 0}\right]\,.

(304)

Here,
1\leq m\leq 2j
, and we use Eq. (
112a
) to reduce the quadratic product
t_{(\alpha)}{t}_{(\beta)}
, as well as the properties listed in Appendix
E
.

For the extremal cases
m=1
and
m=2j
,
Eq. (
304
) reduces to

\displaystyle t_{\alpha_{1}0\cdots 0}t_{\beta_{1}0\cdots 0}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2j}\left[\left(2j-1\right)t_{\alpha_{1}\beta_{1}0\cdots 0}+{\mathcal{R}}_{\alpha_{1}\beta_{1}\xi}t^{\xi}_{0\cdots 0}\right]\,,

(305a)

\displaystyle t_{\alpha_{1}\cdots\alpha_{2j}}t_{\beta_{1}0\cdots 0}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2j}\sum_{n=1}^{2j}{\mathcal{R}}_{\alpha_{n}\beta_{1}\xi}t^{\xi}_{\alpha_{1}\cdots\alpha_{n-1}\alpha_{n+1}\cdots\alpha_{2j}}\,.

(305b)

The following commutation relations are used in Sec.
V.2.3
to prove that every term in the sum of Eq. (
116
) transform covariantly independent from the others,

\displaystyle{\left[t_{\beta,0\cdots 0},t_{\alpha_{1}\cdots\alpha_{m}0\cdots 0}\right]}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{j}{\rm i}\operatorname{Im}\left[\sum_{n=1}^{m}R_{\beta\alpha_{n}\xi}t^{\xi}_{\alpha_{1}\cdots\alpha_{n-1}\alpha_{n+1}\cdots\alpha_{m}0\cdots 0}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{j}\sum_{n=1}^{m}{\rm i}\epsilon_{\beta\rho\alpha_{n}\xi}\,\eta^{\rho}\,t^{\xi}_{\alpha_{1}\cdots\alpha_{n-1}\alpha_{n+1}\cdots\alpha_{m}0\cdots 0}\,,

(306)

where Eqs. (
304
) and (
302
) are used.

The
\mathfrak{sl}(2,\mathbb{C})
multipole of order
m
is defined by Eq. (
155
)

{\mathcal{M}}_{m}^{\mu_{1}\rho_{1},\cdots,\mu_{m}\rho_{m}}=\frac{1}{m!}\underset{\left\{\left(\mu\rho\right)\right\}}{\mathcal{S}}\prod_{r=1}^{m}\mathbb{M}^{\mu_{r}\rho_{r}}-\left({\text{Traces}}\right)\,,

(307)

for which the symmetrized products of spin-
j
representation of Lorentz generators
{\mathbb{M}}^{\mu\rho}
[Eq. (
120
)] are needed.
Using the relation between
t
-tensors and Lorentz generators
{\mathbb{M}}^{\mu\rho}
provided by Eq. (
120
), together with repeated use of Eq. (
304
), one can find a relation between the aforementioned symmetrized products and
t
-tensors.
For example, the first term
m=2

is given by

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2!}\underset{\left\{\left(\mu\rho\right)\right\}}{\mathcal{S}}\prod_{r=1}^{2}\mathbb{M}^{\mu_{r}\rho_{r}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2!}\underset{\left\{\left(\mu\rho\right)\right\}}{\mathcal{S}}\prod_{r=1}^{2}({\rm i}j){\mathcal{Q}}_{\text{red}}^{\mu_{r}\rho_{r}\alpha_{r}}t_{\alpha_{r}0\cdots 0}=\prod_{r=1}^{2}({\rm i}j){\mathcal{Q}}_{\text{red}}^{\mu_{r}\rho_{r}\alpha_{r}}\frac{1}{2!}\underset{\left\{\left(\mu\rho\right)\right\}}{\mathcal{S}}\prod_{l=1}^{2}t_{\alpha_{l}0\cdots 0}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{{\rm i}}{2}\right)^{2}\left[2!\binom{2j}{2}\left[\prod_{r=1}^{2}{\mathcal{Q}}_{\text{red}}^{\mu_{r}\rho_{r}\alpha_{r}}\right]t_{\alpha_{1}\alpha_{2}0\cdots 0}+{\mathcal{C}}_{\text{red}}^{\mu_{1}\rho_{1}\mu_{2}\rho_{2}}t_{0\cdots 0}\right]\,,

(308)

where we have used Eqs. (
305a
), (
301
), (
288a
) and (
292a
).
The next case
m=3
corresponds to

\displaystyle\frac{1}{3!}\underset{\left\{\left(\mu\rho\right)\right\}}{\mathcal{S}}\prod_{r=1}^{3}\mathbb{M}^{\mu_{r}\rho_{r}}=\;

\displaystyle\frac{1}{3!}\underset{\left\{\left(\mu\rho\right)\right\}}{\mathcal{S}}\prod_{r=1}^{3}({\rm i}j){\mathcal{Q}}_{\text{red}}^{\mu_{r}\rho_{r}\alpha_{r}}t_{\alpha_{r}0\cdots 0}

\displaystyle\hskip-14.22636pt=\left(\frac{{\rm i}}{2}\right)^{3}3!\binom{2j}{3}\left[\prod_{r=1}^{3}{\mathcal{Q}}_{\text{red}}^{\mu_{r}\rho_{r}\alpha_{r}}\right]t_{\alpha_{1}\alpha_{2}\alpha_{3}0\cdots 0}-\left(\frac{{\rm i}}{2}\right)^{3}\frac{(3j-1)2j}{3}\underset{\left\{\left(\mu\rho\right)\right\}}{\mathcal{S}}{\mathcal{C}}_{\text{red}}^{\mu_{1}\rho_{1}\mu_{2}\rho_{2}}{\mathcal{Q}}_{\text{red}}^{\mu_{3}\rho_{3}\alpha_{3}}t_{\alpha_{3}0\cdots 0}\,.

(309)

The pattern continues for higher order terms, which take the general form

\displaystyle\frac{1}{m!}\underset{\left\{\left(\mu\rho\right)\right\}}{\mathcal{S}}\prod_{r=1}^{m}\mathbb{M}^{\mu_{r}\rho_{r}}=

\displaystyle\left(\frac{{\rm i}}{2}\right)^{m}m!\binom{2j}{m}\mathsf{T}{}_{m}^{(\mu\rho)}+\sum_{n=1}^{\lfloor\frac{m}{2}\rfloor}d_{m,n}\underset{\left\{\left(\mu\rho\right)\right\}}{\mathcal{S}}\mathsf{T}{}_{m-2n}^{(\mu\rho)}\prod_{l=m-2n+1,m-2n+3,\cdots}^{m-1}{\mathcal{C}}_{\text{red}}^{\mu_{l}\rho_{l}\mu_{l+1}\rho_{l+1}}\,,

(310)

where we use the notation introduced in Eq. (
119a
).
We did not find a simple closed form for the coefficients
d_{m,n}
. However, that does not concern us here as we still need to subtract the traces to get to the desired result of Eq. (
307
). The important point is that all the terms of the sum in the right-hand side of Eq. (
310
) will not survive these trace subtractions and will be absorbed (partially) in the
\mathcal{T}_{m}^{(\mu\nu)}
.
These terms have the same form as the
{\mathcal{C}}_{\text{red}}
-
traces
we found in Sec.
V.2.4
.
This shows that the
\mathfrak{sl}(2,\mathbb{C})
multipoles must be
{\mathcal{C}}_{\text{red}}
-
traceless
in the sense of Eq. (
148
), and they are related to our orthogonal basis
\mathcal{T}_{m}^{(\mu\rho)}
[see Eq. (
147
)] only by a multiplicative factor.
Moreover, the multiplicative factor is that of the highest-order term
\mathsf{T}{}_{m}^{(\mu\rho)}
in Eq. (
310
), which is the one containing the non-trace part of the symmetrized product of Lorentz generators.
We conclude that the
m
th multipoles are given by

{\mathcal{M}}_{m}^{\mu_{1}\rho_{1},\cdots,\mu_{m}\rho_{m}}=\frac{{\rm i}^{m}}{2^{m}}m!\binom{2j}{m}{\mathcal{T}}_{m}^{\mu_{1}\rho_{1},\cdots,\mu_{m}\rho_{m}}\,,\qquad 0\leq m\leq 2j\,.

(311)

It is worth emphasizing that the subtracted traces appearing in the definition of
{\mathcal{T}}_{m}^{(\mu\rho)}
(right-hand side of Eq. (
311
)) are not related by an overall multiplicative factor with the traces in the definition of the covariant multipoles Eq. (
307
). The reason is that the symmetrized product of Lorentz generators already contains trace-like terms with multiplicative factors that are term dependent, see Eq. (
310
).

As usual, all the corresponding expressions for the right-handed chiral representation are obtained by
barring
those given for the left-handed one.
